# DevFathom: business features in plain words (SuperAdmin is in its own file)

Last updated 10 November 2026 (PKT). This covers the current development version of DevFathom (develop, as of 7 November 2026, code version `1906b530`). The previous edition covered 1 November. DevFathom serves four kinds of business: **agencies** (studios and firms), **restaurants**, **pharmacies** and **hotels**. All four are run from one DevFathom admin console.

**What the labels mean**
- **Available now**: built and in the current development version. **This doesn't mean testing has finished**: our QA team checks features separately, and we flag known problems.
- **New**: added since the 1 November edition.
- **Built, waiting to be merged**: built and waiting for review (an open pull request).
- **Decided, not built yet**: the owner has decided how it should work, but nobody has built it.
- **Planned, not built yet**: agreed, but not built yet.

**Please note:** no one has yet checked that one business can never see another business's data, and the DevFathom admin console hasn't been tested by our QA team yet. Our examples use businesses in Lahore and across Punjab.

**What's new since 1 November, in short**
- **Ask Fathom** now has its own AI routing (Claude first, then OpenAI, then Amazon Bedrock) with monthly spending limits, and the "fails on every question" fault has a fix. It still needs a fresh test before we call it working.
- **Pharmacies** get procedures (checklists with proof), a switch for supplier licence checks, reading batch and expiry from a photo of a pack at goods-in, live CCTV viewing and camera snapshots as evidence, and a redesigned counter screen.
- **Pharmacy permissions are tighter:** buying, the cash drawer, stock by branch, side-effect reports and patient records now each check the person's role and branch.
- **Restaurants:** voiding an item or cancelling an order with an approver now needs the approver's own PIN or badge.
- **Agencies:** each agency now numbers its invoices on its own.
- **Two owner decisions are still not built:** manager or owner PIN approval for refunds, and the narcotic flag for pharmacist review.

---

## 2. Shared features (every business gets these)

**Your organisation settings (Available now).** The business's administrator sets its name and logo, adds departments (for example Kitchen, Front of House and Accounts), creates custom staff roles with exactly the permissions they want, adds staff directly or resets a staff member's password, connects their web address and email sender, and turns the AI assistant on or off.

**Signing in safely (Available now).** Sign in with email and password, reset a forgotten password by email, and switch on a phone code (two-step sign-in) with backup codes. Restaurants also have a quick till sign-in. Large customers can use their company login. *Example:* a Gulberg agency owner switches on two-step sign-in, so a stolen password alone can't open her account.

**Ask Fathom, the AI assistant (Available now; the fix for "every question fails" is in, but not yet re-tested).** Staff ask questions in plain language, and the assistant can do simple tasks, always within that person's permissions. Any change it suggests is saved only after the person confirms it, and only once. It answers with figures taken from your own records. Behind the scenes it tries Anthropic's Claude first, then OpenAI, then Amazon Bedrock if a provider is down. It never uses Azure by default. Saad ordered Azure removed from all code on 10 Nov 2026; this is planned for the next Codex batch (DEV-755 / #1437). Everyday questions use the standard model. The most powerful model writes the weekly owner summary, and handles deep strategy questions only if your business has opted in. DevFathom sets a monthly spending limit for each AI provider, and our staff are warned at 80%. The Ask Fathom button now appears only once the system has confirmed it's available for your business, so you won't see a button stuck on "checking". AI stays switched off on a server until DevFathom turns it on. Planned: monthly AI credits shown to each owner, and suggestion panels in reports. *Example:* the owner of a pharmacy on Ferozepur Road asks "Which items lost me sales last week?" and gets an answer built from her own counter records. If Claude is busy, the same question goes to the next provider without her noticing.

**Connecting other software (Available now).** Developers can use DevFathom's API with access keys that can be revoked, and can send events to other systems through webhooks, with a record of every delivery. Other shared features:
- search across everything
- in-app notifications
- file uploads
- live updates
- video meetings
- phone wallet loyalty cards
- CCTV camera connections (for restaurants and pharmacies)

Some of these need outside services set up first.

The team, HR, chat, notes, meetings and email features below are the same for every business.

## 3. Pharmacies

Who is who: the *Owner* runs the business, the *Pharmacist* checks and hands out medicines, the *Manager* runs a branch and the *Cashier* serves at the counter. There are also Purchasing, Stock, Finance and Auditor roles. Every feature below says what it is, how it works, who can use it (taken from the permission check in the code), the limits the code enforces, and a Lahore or Punjab example. The "who can use it" lines were written on 2 November; rows changed since then were rechecked on 10 November. *Fixed since 1 November:* buying, the cash drawer, stock by branch, side-effect reports and patient records now check the person's role and branch, not just that they're signed in. *Still open:* editing the medicine catalogue and running marketing campaigns still only check that the feature is switched on, so a cashier could reach them. Fixes are in review.

### 1. POS and sales

**Counter sale: search, scan, cart, lot pick, tender, receipt (Available now, with a new layout).** The main till screen. The cashier searches by name or scans a barcode, adds medicines to the cart, picks which batch (lot) to sell from, takes payment (cash, card, wallet or khata) and prints or shows a receipt. The screen was redesigned in November. A header shows the branch, the drawer state and buttons for returns and orders. Small tags above the cart show the patient and prescription, any safety blocks, and the customer's loyalty or khata status. On a phone, a "Pay" bar stays at the bottom of the screen. The checks behind the screen didn't change, with two exceptions. The system now re-checks at payment, not only when the item was added, that a batch hasn't expired, been recalled or been put on hold. And the ready-made Cashier role now works at the till without any extra settings. Who uses it: staff with the counter permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner, Owner / Operator, Cashier) who are assigned to that branch. *Example:* at a Johar Town pharmacy, a cashier adds a strip of tablets at 9:58 pm. At 10:00 pm that batch is recalled. When she presses Pay, the till stops the sale and asks her to pick another batch.

**Pack authentication by scan at the counter (Available now).** Scanning the barcode or code on a pack at the till to check the pack is genuine and matches what is in stock. How it works: behind the screen the system has 1 server action that save changes, each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log. Key settings and limits checked in code: payload up to 512 characters or units. *Example:* A customer in Model Town buys an imported insulin pen; the pharmacist scans the pack code and the system confirms it matches a lot received from the authorised distributor.

**Custom keyboard shortcuts per cashier (Available now).** Each cashier can set their own keyboard shortcuts for till actions such as "pay cash" or "new sale". How it works: behind the screen the system has 2 server actions (1 that read and 1 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code). Key settings and limits checked in code: key up to 16 characters or units. *Example:* A fast cashier at a busy Anarkali pharmacy sets F2 for "cash payment" so she never has to touch the mouse during the evening rush.

**Receipt format and printing (browser print and print jobs) (Available now).** Controls how receipts look and lets the till print through the browser or send a print job to a receipt printer. How it works: behind the screen the system has 2 server actions that only read data, each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: signed-in staff who also pass an extra permission or branch check written inside that action. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “This bill is not settled, so it has no receipt to print.”. *Example:* A Multan branch sets its receipt to show the NTN and branch phone number and prints on a 80 mm thermal printer.

**Manual payment settings (bank or wallet details) and a payment sandbox (Available now).** Where the pharmacy saves its bank account or mobile-wallet details (for example JazzCash or Easypaisa) so cashiers can take manual transfers; a test-payment sandbox exists for now. How it works: behind the screen the system has 2 server actions (1 that read and 1 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; signed-in staff who also pass an extra permission or branch check written inside that action. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A DHA Lahore pharmacy saves its Meezan Bank IBAN and Easypaisa number so customers can pay by transfer and the cashier marks the payment as received.

**Cash drawer: open, cash in and out, close (Available now; tightened in November).** The cash drawer for each till: open it with a starting float, record cash put in or taken out with a reason, and close it with a count. Opening, cash in or out and closing now need the counter permission, and the person must be assigned to that branch. A "deny" set for one person always wins over what their role allows. The owner's decision that only a manager or the owner may close a drawer is still waiting for review. Who uses it: staff with the counter permission who are assigned to the branch, such as cashiers, Owner / Operators and owners. *Example:* a stock helper at a Shadman pharmacy, who has no counter permission, tries to record Rs 2,000 "paid out" from the drawer. The system refuses, and the drawer total stays correct.

**Day close: preview, close and evidence (Available now).** End-of-day close: preview the day's totals, enter the counted cash, explain any difference, close the day and keep the evidence. How it works: behind the screen the system has 3 server actions (1 that read and 2 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: signed-in staff who also pass an extra permission or branch check written inside that action. Key settings and limits checked in code: variance note up to 1,000 characters or units. *Checked in code (2 Nov 2026):* the role gap is closed on develop. Preview, close and the daily-close record now require an administrator or someone holding the day-close permission (“Day-close permission is required to reconcile and close a day.”). The evidence export is administrators only. *Example:* At 11 pm the Gulberg branch manager previews the day, counts Rs 182,400 against Rs 182,650 expected, writes "Rs 250 short, change error" and closes the day.

**Returns and refunds with receipt lookup (Available now).** Find an old sale by its receipt number, return one or more lines, and refund by the original payment method. A history of returns is kept. Returns and refunds need the counter permission at that branch. Returning a controlled medicine also needs a witness to enter their PIN. Today there is **no** step where a manager or owner approves a refund; see the next item. *Example:* a customer in Faisalabad brings back an unopened box of antibiotics with the receipt. The cashier looks up the receipt, returns the box and refunds the cash. The refund shows in the day's totals.

**Refund approval by manager or owner PIN (Decided, not built yet).** The owner has decided that only a manager or the owner can approve a refund, by entering their own PIN, so every refund carries the approver's name. We checked the code on 10 November: refunds still need only the counter permission, and nobody has built the PIN step. Until it's built, use a written refund log and the daily close review. *Example:* once built, a cashier in Sialkot who starts a Rs 3,000 refund will need the branch manager to enter his PIN before cash leaves the drawer.

**Offline counter (device enrolment, lease, replay, quota) (Available now).** Lets the till keep selling when the internet drops: the device is enrolled, gets a time-limited lease and stock quota, and uploads the stored sales when back online. How it works: behind the screen the system has 3 server actions that save changes, each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; administrators who also pass an extra permission or branch check written inside that action. Key settings and limits checked in code: name up to 100 characters or units, signature up to 200 characters or units, snapshots up to 8 characters or units, quantity up to 1,000 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “invalid_snapshot”; “command_rejected”; “invalid_manual_capture”. *Example:* During a PTCL outage in Bahawalpur the counter keeps selling within its quota and the sales upload automatically when the connection returns.

**Customer ordering and delivery (place, cancel, staff queue, runners, checkout) (Available now).** Customers place orders for pickup or delivery; staff see a queue, assign riders and check the order out at the till; customers can cancel. How it works: behind the screen the system has 12 server actions (6 that read and 6 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “pharmacy:orders” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner, Owner / Operator, Manager, Cashier); the feature must also be switched on for the business; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code). Key settings and limits checked in code: search up to 120 characters or units, address line up to 300 characters or units, city up to 120 characters or units, delivery instructions up to 500 characters or units. *Example:* A family in Wapda Town orders monthly BP medicines for home delivery; staff pack the order and assign a rider.

**Lost-sale demand capture and restock interest (tell the customer when stock is back, opt-out) (Available now).** Records when a customer asks for something out of stock, and can tell the customer when it is back (they can opt out). How it works: behind the screen the system has 6 server actions (2 that read and 4 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code). Key settings and limits checked in code: campaign key up to 160 characters or units. *Example:* Three people in Rawalpindi ask for a children's syrup that is out of stock; the manager sees the lost demand and the customers get a message when it arrives.

**Substitution and upsell suggestions, duplicate-salt warning (Available now).** Suggests an equivalent medicine or add-on and warns when two items in the cart contain the same active salt. How it works: behind the screen the system has 4 server actions (2 that read and 2 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code); signed-in staff who also pass an extra permission or branch check written inside that action. Key settings and limits checked in code: medicine ids up to 128 characters or units, limit up to 20 characters or units, ingredient key up to 200 characters or units. *Example:* A cashier adds two different paracetamol brands; the till warns about the duplicate salt before the sale.

**Settlement controls shown only to people with the capability (Built, waiting to be merged).** Hides settlement buttons from staff who do not hold the right permission. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* After this is merged, a new cashier in Gujranwala would not see the settlement controls at all.

**Direct sign-out from Counter (Built, waiting to be merged).** A sign-out button directly on the counter screen. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A cashier at shift change in Cantt, Lahore signs out from the till in one tap so the next person signs in.

**Tenant currency in day-close totals (Built, waiting to be merged).** Day-close totals shown in the business's own currency setting. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A pharmacy set to PKR sees "Rs" on its day-close figures instead of a default currency.

**Readable mobile receipts / responsive layout (Built, waiting to be merged).** Receipts and the counter layout that read properly on phones and small screens. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A customer in Township receives a receipt link on WhatsApp that is readable on a small phone.

**Faster counter scan and search (Built, waiting to be merged).** Makes scanning and searching at the till noticeably faster on a busy counter. A fix is now in review. *Example:* during the evening rush at an Anarkali pharmacy, a scanned barcode should land in the cart straight away instead of after a pause.

**More counter improvements (Built, waiting to be merged).** These are built and waiting for review: recording a khata repayment at the counter; wallet payments entered by hand; brand names shown in search results; a quantity box you can clear and retype; a working shortcuts selector; the receipt staying on screen after payment; faster payment with quick-cash buttons; pharmacists and cashiers taking payment for a prescription bill; the server itself refusing prescription-only medicines without a prescription; and a printed receipt with the pharmacy's address and licence number. *Example:* a regular customer in Samanabad pays Rs 1,500 off his khata balance, and the cashier records it at the till instead of in a notebook.


### 2. Dispensing and prescriptions

**Patients, consent, prescription uploads and reading, extraction, capture, fill (Available now).** Patient records with consent, prescription photo uploads, reading and extracting the medicines on the prescription, and filling it. How it works: behind the screen the system has 13 server actions (6 that read and 7 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log; signed-in staff who also pass an extra permission or branch check written inside that action; staff whose role carries the “pharmacy:dispense” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner, Owner / Operator, Pharmacist); the feature must also be switched on for the business; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code); staff with access to this part of the system (gate set by a shared helper: publicProcedure). Key settings and limits checked in code: display name up to 200 characters or units, cnic up to 32 characters or units, phone up to 32 characters or units, patient id up to 200 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Pharmacy branch not found.”; “Patient not found.”; “Prescription reading is not enabled for this workspace.”. *Example:* A patient in Gulberg uploads a photo of a doctor's prescription; the pharmacist checks the extracted medicines and fills it.

**Patient records: view and export (Available now; tightened in November).** Open one patient's record or export it, including consents, prescriptions and fills. Both now need the prescriptions permission, which only the pharmacist-type roles hold; a wildcard "all permissions" grant doesn't include it. The export hides most of the CNIC and phone number. Who uses it: pharmacists, Owner / Operators, and anyone given the prescriptions permission by name. *Example:* a Gulberg pharmacist exports a patient's record for a doctor's referral. The file shows the prescriptions, while the CNIC appears as 35202-XXXXXXX-1. A cashier who tries the same thing is refused.

**Refill reminders (capture) (Available now).** Notes when a patient will need a refill so they can be reminded. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A diabetic patient in Samanabad buys a 30-day supply and the pharmacy records a refill reminder for day 27.

**Counter patient panel (Available now).** A side panel at the till showing the linked patient's details and history. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* While serving a regular in Allama Iqbal Town, the cashier sees his last purchases and allergies in the panel.

**Rx-only sale gate (a prescription reference is needed) (Available now).** Stops sale of prescription-only medicines unless a prescription reference is entered. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A cashier in Okara tries to sell an antibiotic without a prescription and the till refuses until the prescription number is entered.

**Pharmacist queue / dispensing (Available now).** The pharmacist's work queue: review requested items, then verify, clarify, refuse, override, counsel or sign; and record substitutions. How it works: behind the screen the system has 6 server actions (3 that read and 3 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “pharmacy:dispense” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner, Owner / Operator, Pharmacist); the feature must also be switched on for the business. *Checked in code (2 Nov 2026):* every action needs the dispensing permission. The pharmacist can verify, clarify, refuse, override, counsel or sign. Refusals and overrides do not have to give a reason. The code does not stop the person who queued an item from also signing it. The queue shows every item in the business: it is not filtered by branch or by open status, and it has no paging. The role-template note says self-signing is not allowed, but the code does not enforce that. *Example:* At a 24-hour pharmacy near Services Hospital, the pharmacist verifies a queued prescription line and signs it before it is handed over.

**Medicine-name search in prescription capture (Built, waiting to be merged).** Medicine-name search inside prescription capture. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* When typing a prescription in Multan, the pharmacist types "augm" and picks Augmentin 625 from the list.

**Capability checks for patient and consent (Built, waiting to be merged).** Permission checks on patient and consent records. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* After this fix, a cashier in Sargodha without patient access could not open consent records.

**Patient denial test coverage (Built, waiting to be merged).** Automated tests that prove patient data is refused to the wrong people. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* Developers check that a Gujranwala cashier is denied another branch's patient list.


### 3. Inventory and batches

**Stock on hand, stock signals dashboard and digest (Available now; branch rules added in November).** Live stock levels by branch, warning signals (low, expiring or slow-moving stock) on a dashboard, and a summary digest. Staff now see a branch's stock only if they're assigned to that branch. The business's administrator can see every branch. Anyone else gets a clear "you need a branch assignment" message. The same rule covers counts, write-offs, receiving lots and every step of a branch transfer. *Example:* a stock clerk assigned to the DHA branch opens stock on hand and sees only DHA. The owner sees DHA and Model Town side by side.

**Medicine catalogue: ingredients, medicines, pack levels, printed MRP, loose-unit price, barcodes, aliases, scan resolve (Available now).** The medicine list: active ingredients, brands, pack levels (pack, strip, unit), printed MRP, loose-unit price, barcodes, other names and scan lookup. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). *Known problem:* Sev1 gap on edits (module-only). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A pharmacy in Faisalabad adds a new brand with its printed MRP of Rs 450 a pack and a per-tablet loose price.

**Price notices, price confirmation, pack prices and overrides (Available now).** Price-change notices, confirming new prices, and pack prices with overrides. How it works: behind the screen the system has 4 server actions (2 that read and 2 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; staff whose role carries the “pharmacy:safety” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner, Owner / Operator); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Key settings and limits checked in code: proposal ids up to 100 characters or units. *Example:* When a manufacturer raises an MRP, the Lahore owner gets a notice and confirms the new price before the till uses it.

**Reference catalogue connector (proposals and decisions) (Available now).** Proposals from a shared reference medicine catalogue that the pharmacy can accept or reject. How it works: behind the screen the system has 2 server actions that save changes, each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; staff whose role carries the “pharmacy:catalogue-review” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner, Owner / Operator, Pharmacist); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Key settings and limits checked in code: label up to 200 characters or units, expression up to 120 characters or units, timezone up to 100 characters or units, proposal id up to 200 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Connector proposal not found.”; “Connector proposal already has a pharmacist decision.”; “Target medicine not found.”. *Example:* A Bahawalpur pharmacy accepts a reference proposal that adds the correct barcode for a common syrup.

**Duplicate detection and catalogue review decisions (Available now).** Finds duplicate medicines and records review decisions on catalogue changes. How it works: behind the screen the system has 3 server actions (1 that read and 2 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Review references must be unique.”; “Review candidate or assertion not found.”; “Review assertions must be unreviewed and belong to the referenced candidates.”. *Example:* The system notices "Brufen 400" was entered twice in Rawalpindi and the reviewer merges them.

**Staged catalogue import review (Available now).** Imported catalogue rows are staged so someone reviews them before they go live. How it works: behind the screen the system has 4 server actions that only read data, each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only. Key settings and limits checked in code: page up to 10,000 characters or units, row id up to 128 characters or units. *Example:* A new Sialkot pharmacy uploads 3,000 products and an admin reviews the staged rows first.

**Opening-stock import (upload, preview, commit, history) (Available now).** Upload opening stock, preview it, commit it and see the import history. How it works: behind the screen the system has 4 server actions (1 that read and 3 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* On go-live day in Johar Town the owner uploads a spreadsheet of opening stock with batches and expiry dates.

**Branch transfers (Available now).** Move stock between branches: request it, approve it, send it and receive it, with any shortfall recorded. The transfer board now needs the stock permission, and each step needs an assignment to the branch doing it. *Example:* Model Town sends 20 boxes of ORS to the Township branch. Township receives 18 and records 2 as damaged in transit, so both branches' stock stays right.

**Expiry board and near-expiry lots (Available now).** A board of batches that are close to expiry. How it works: behind the screen the system has 2 server actions that only read data, each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The Multan manager sees 14 lots expiring within 90 days and plans returns.

**Expiry advice / expiry returns / work-pack expiry evidence (Available now; fix merged, not yet re-tested).** Advice on what to do with stock that's about to expire, expiry returns to suppliers, and evidence packs. The database fault that broke this was fixed in November, but our QA team hasn't re-tested it yet. Still in review: clearer error messages, a bounded owner view, and a near-expiry list grouped by distributor with a "draft return claim" button. *Example:* a Bahawalpur pharmacy sees 40 strips of a syrup expiring in six weeks. The advice suggests returning them to the distributor before the claim window closes.

**Temperature zones / cold chain (Available now).** Temperature zones (for example fridge) for cold-chain medicines. How it works: behind the screen the system has 1 server action that only read data, each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A pharmacy in Faisalabad marks insulin and vaccines as "2–8 °C fridge" stock.

**DRAP recall actions and recall trace, recall write-off guard (Available now).** Acts on DRAP recall notices, traces which batches were sold or are in stock, and stops wrongly writing off recalled stock. How it works: behind the screen the system has 2 server actions that only read data, each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* When DRAP recalls a cough-syrup batch, the Lahore owner sees which branch holds it and who bought it.

**Catalogue edits go through review (separation of duties) (Built, waiting to be merged).** Catalogue edits are proposed by one person and approved by another. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* Once merged, a Gujranwala cashier's price edit would wait for the owner's approval.

**Stock permission plus branch assignment (Available now, new).** Stock screens and actions need both the stock permission and an assignment to the branch. The business administrator is the only exception. This used to be waiting for review and is now in the development version. See "Stock on hand" above for how it works. *Example:* a new stock clerk in Rawalpindi can't see any stock until the owner assigns her to the Saddar branch.

**Default catalogue for new pharmacies (Built, waiting to be merged).** A starter medicine catalogue for new pharmacies. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A new pharmacy in Okara would start with common medicines already listed.

**Pack and barcode edits (Built, waiting to be merged).** Editing pack and barcode details. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A manager in Rawalpindi fixes a wrong barcode on a strip pack.

**Pharmacy record retention periods (Built, waiting to be merged).** How long pharmacy records are kept. The PR failed security review because one global lock would make all tenants wait. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. *Known problem:* failed security (a global lock serializes every tenant). Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A Lahore chain would set how many years sales records are kept, once fixed and merged.

**Narcotic flag for pharmacist review (Decided, not built yet).** The owner has decided that narcotic medicines should be flagged so a pharmacist reviews them. We checked the code on 10 November, and there is no such flag yet. What already exists is separate: a medicine marked as *controlled* can only be sold with the patient's address, a second pharmacist's PIN, and a prescription or recorded emergency supply, and every sale goes into the controlled-drugs register. *Example:* once built, when a pharmacy in Gujranwala adds a new codeine syrup to its catalogue, it will be flagged automatically, and a pharmacist must review it before anyone can sell it.


### 4. Purchasing and goods-in

**Suppliers, supplier licences and authorization (Available now; tightened in November).** Keep supplier records, their drug-sale licences and whether each is authorised to supply you. Recording a supplier now needs the purchasing permission. A licence is checked by a second step that records who filed it and who verified it separately, so nobody can quietly mark their own upload as verified. *Example:* a Lahore pharmacy's purchasing officer uploads a distributor's licence. The manager opens it, checks the expiry date and marks it verified, and both names are kept.

**Supplier licence and authorisation checks: on/off switch (Available now, new).** One setting lets the owner or administrator turn off the two supplier checks: licence on file, and authorised to trade. It's **on** by default, and if the setting can't be read, the checks stay on. When it's off, buying and receiving no longer ask for these papers, suppliers show "Not checked" (never "Verified"), and the screen reminds the owner that the pharmacy is still legally responsible for buying from licensed sources. Controlled-drug safeguards don't change: the pharmacist witness PIN at goods-in, the register and the counter checks all still apply. *Example:* a small family pharmacy in Okara buys only from two well-known distributors and hasn't collected their licences yet. The owner switches the checks off for now, and every supplier shows "Not checked" until the papers are added.

**Requisitions, quotes, quote comparison (Available now).** Purchase requests, supplier quotes and side-by-side quote comparison. How it works: behind the screen the system has 4 server actions (1 that read and 3 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code). Key settings and limits checked in code: reference up to 100 characters or units, branch id up to 200 characters or units, note up to 2,000 characters or units, medicine id up to 200 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “A scheme needs both a buy quantity and a free quantity.”; “A quote cannot expire before it was given.”. *Example:* The Faisalabad owner gets three quotes for ORS and picks the cheapest licensed supplier.

**Purchase orders: create, submit, approve, send, landed cost (Available now; tightened in November).** Create, submit, approve and send purchase orders, with the landed cost (price plus freight and other charges). Raising an order needs the purchasing permission. Approving and sending need a separate approval permission, so the person who asked for the goods can't approve their own order. The ready-made Manager and Owner / Operator roles have the approval permission. A pharmacy run by the owner alone can approve its own order after entering a fresh PIN, and that approval is recorded. *Example:* in a Wapda Town pharmacy, the purchasing clerk raises a Rs 85,000 order, and the manager approves it. In a one-person shop in Kasur, the owner approves his own order with his PIN.

**Goods receiving: post, reverse, scan, pack authentication, exceptions queue (Available now).** Receive goods against a purchase order, reverse mistakes, scan packs, check they're genuine, and work through a queue of problems. It now needs the purchasing permission at that branch. *Example:* a Johar Town receiver scans 30 boxes against an order, finds 2 boxes with the wrong batch, and sends them to the exceptions queue for the supplier.

**Read batch and expiry from a photo of a pack (Available now, new; switched off until DevFathom turns it on).** While receiving against an order, the receiver takes a photo of one pack. The system suggests the batch number and expiry date, which the receiver can correct. She then types how many packs share them, and can pick a shelf. The photo only fills in the line; stock moves only when the receipt is reviewed and posted as normal. The photo is never stored. Limits: 20 photos a minute per person and 60 per business, and it doesn't run on demo accounts. *Example:* a delivery of 48 boxes of the same antibiotic arrives at a Gulberg pharmacy. The receiver photographs one box, checks the suggested batch and expiry, types 48, and the line is filled in seconds.

**Controlled-drug witnesses at goods-in (co-auth; the second checker must hold dispense) (Available now).** For controlled drugs, a second person who holds the dispensing permission must co-sign the goods-in. How it works: behind the screen the system has 1 server action that only read data, each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: signed-in staff who also pass an extra permission or branch check written inside that action. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* When morphine arrives at a hospital pharmacy in Lahore, the pharmacist co-signs the receipt.

**Purchase returns, debit notes, supplier credits (Available now).** Returning stock to suppliers with debit notes and recording supplier credits. How it works: behind the screen the system has 2 server actions that save changes, each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code). Key settings and limits checked in code: reference up to 120 characters or units, note up to 1,000 characters or units, reason up to 500 characters or units. *Example:* A Sialkot pharmacy returns short-dated stock and records the supplier's credit note.

**Central (multi-branch) purchasing (Available now).** Buying centrally for several branches. How it works: behind the screen the system has 3 server actions (1 that read and 2 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log. Key settings and limits checked in code: reference up to 100 characters or units. *Example:* A chain head office in Lahore orders for all five branches in one PO.

**Supplier portal links (issue, revoke, document upload, proposals) (Available now; gap fixed).** Secure links that let a supplier open an order, upload documents and send proposals without an account. The staff side (making, cancelling and approving links) now needs the purchasing permission at the branch, which closes the gap we reported on 2 November. *Example:* a Lahore pharmacy sends its distributor a link to confirm an order. The distributor uploads the invoice through the link, and only purchasing staff can approve it.

**Role and branch checks for all buying (Available now, new).** Every buying action now refuses by default unless the person holds the right permission and works at that branch, with approval kept separate from buying. This was waiting for review on 2 November and is now in the development version. Our QA team hasn't tested it yet. *Example:* a cashier at a Faisalabad branch opens the purchasing page and sees only what her role allows; she can't create or approve an order.

**Solo owners approve their own orders with a PIN (Available now, new).** When the owner is the only active staff member, they can approve their own purchase order after entering a fresh PIN, and the approval is recorded. In any business with other staff, someone else must approve. They set their PIN from the purchasing screen. *Example:* the owner of a one-counter pharmacy in Sheikhupura raises and approves a small order on a Sunday, entering his PIN to confirm.

**Goods-receipt branch check before the PO is locked (Built, waiting to be merged).** Checks the receiving branch before a PO is locked. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A Bahawalpur PO could not be received into the wrong branch.

**Returns access for procurement roles (Built, waiting to be merged).** Lets procurement roles handle returns. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A purchaser in Lahore could process supplier returns without being an admin.

**Supplier-return claim journal (Built, waiting to be merged).** A journal for supplier-return claims. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A Faisalabad pharmacy tracks its claim against a distributor for returned stock.

**Branch-limited staff can't touch other branches' POs (Built, waiting to be merged).** Branch-limited staff can't touch other branches' POs. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A Model Town manager could not edit the DHA branch's POs.

**Shared PO eligibility validation, partial invoice balances (Built, waiting to be merged).** Shared PO validation and partial invoice balances. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A Gujranwala pharmacy pays half a supplier invoice and the balance stays open.

**Live search on goods-in, and shorter order lines (Built, waiting to be merged).** Goods-in search will show results as you type. Order lines will show only the fields you need, so the phone screen isn't 14 screens of scrolling. *Example:* a receiver in Gujrat types "pana" and sees Panadol at once, without pressing Enter.


### 5. Customers and loyalty

**Customer directory, link a customer to a sale, customer snapshot (Available now).** Customer list, attaching a customer to a sale and a quick summary of that customer. How it works: behind the screen the system has 3 server actions (2 that read and 1 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code). Key settings and limits checked in code: query up to 100 characters or units, display name up to 200 characters or units, page up to 10,000 characters or units. *Example:* A regular in Garden Town is linked to her sale so her history is kept.

**Loyalty points redemption (Available now).** Customers redeem loyalty points at the till. How it works: behind the screen the system has 1 server action that save changes, each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “pharmacy:dispense” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner, Owner / Operator, Pharmacist); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Key settings and limits checked in code: idempotency key up to 160 characters or units. *Example:* A customer in Johar Town uses 500 points for Rs 50 off.

**Khata (customer credit) accounts and statements (Available now).** Khata (credit) accounts for trusted customers, with statements. How it works: behind the screen the system has 2 server actions that only read data, each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A shopkeeper in Shahdara buys on credit and gets a monthly statement.

**Khata WhatsApp reminders (Available now).** WhatsApp reminders for unpaid khata balances. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Lahore pharmacy reminds a customer that Rs 4,200 is due.

**Khata Urdu templates and language choice (Built, waiting to be merged).** Urdu khata reminder templates and a language choice. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A customer in Kasur would receive the reminder in Urdu.


### 6. HR and roles

**Ready-made pharmacy roles (Available now).** Each new pharmacy starts with these roles. *Owner*: everything in the pharmacy area, both reports, and side-effect reporting, but not dispensing or catalogue review, which stay with pharmacists. *Owner / Operator*: for a proprietor who also works the counter, with 12 named permissions, including approving purchase orders. *Pharmacist*: dispense, review the catalogue and close the day. *Manager*: run the branch, orders, buying and approving orders. *Cashier*: counter and orders. *Purchasing*, *Stock*, *Finance* and *Auditor* have one area each. New pharmacies also get procedure (checklist) permissions on each role. The person who sets up a new pharmacy can report side effects and is assigned to the main branch from day one. *Example:* a new pharmacy in Multan is set up. The owner adds his brother as Manager and two staff as Cashiers, with no permission settings needed.

**Separated permissions for sensitive work (Available now).** Four permissions are kept apart and must be given by name: dispensing, prescription records, side-effect (safety) reporting and catalogue review. A general "all permissions" grant doesn't include them, so an administrator doesn't automatically become a pharmacist. *Example:* the administrator of a Lahore chain can manage staff and prices, but can't sign off a dispensed prescription unless she is also given the dispensing permission.

**Org roles, custom roles, deny overrides (ABAC) (Available now).** Business roles, custom roles and deny overrides. How it works: behind the screen the system has 19 server actions (4 that read and 15 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code). Key settings and limits checked in code: name up to 100 characters or units, sort order up to 10,000 characters or units, display name up to 150 characters or units, locale up to 20 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Workspace not found.”; “Administrator access is required.”; “Department not found.”. *Example:* A Faisalabad owner creates a "Night cashier" role and denies refunds to it.

**Branch assignments and branch scope (Available now).** Assigning staff to branches and limiting what they see to those branches. How it works: behind the screen the system has 3 server actions (1 that read and 2 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only. Key settings and limits checked in code: branch id up to 128 characters or units, user id up to 128 characters or units, assignment id up to 128 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Branch is outside your scope.”; “That member cannot be assigned to a pharmacy branch.”; “Assignment was not found.”. *Example:* A pharmacist assigned to the Gulberg branch only sees Gulberg data.

**Menus that match your access (Available now, new).** The pharmacy menu now asks the system which areas you can open, so you only see links that will actually work for you. *Example:* a cashier in Sahiwal no longer sees a "Purchasing" link that would only show an error.

**Team, invites, last-admin guard (Available now).** Team list, invitations and a guard so the last admin can't be removed. How it works: behind the screen the system has 28 server actions (11 that read and 17 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; staff with access to this part of the system (gate set by a shared helper: hrProcedure); staff whose role carries the “leave:approve” permission; the feature must also be switched on for the business; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code); signed-in staff who also pass an extra permission or branch check written inside that action. Key settings and limits checked in code: organization role id up to 128 characters or units, limit up to 1,000 characters or units, year up to 2,100 characters or units, entitled days up to 366 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Invalid department.”; “Invalid role template.”; “A user with this email already exists”. *Example:* The Rawalpindi owner invites a new pharmacist by email.

**Role-based dashboards (Available now).** Dashboards that differ by role. Bug: a cashier can't save a layout and sees the owner's title. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). *Known problem:* bug: a cashier can't save a layout (403) and sees "Owner command centre" (DEV-602). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Lahore cashier sees her sales dashboard (but currently gets an error saving it).

**Updated role templates (pharmacist sells, takes payment and opens the drawer; manager closes the day) (Built, waiting to be merged).** Updated role templates where the pharmacist can sell and open the drawer and the manager closes the day. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* After merge, a pharmacist in Sialkot could also take payments at quiet times.

**A "deny" always wins (Available now).** If a person's own settings deny something, that beats anything their role allows. The development version now works this way, and the cash drawer and stock checks rely on it. A larger security change with the same name is still in review. *Example:* an owner in Bahawalnagar blocks one cashier from the drawer. Even though the Cashier role normally allows it, she can't open the drawer.

**Device co-approval; device revoke needs `devices:revoke` (Built, waiting to be merged).** Two people approve a new device; revoking needs a special permission. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* After merge, a new till tablet in DHA would need two approvals.

**OTP login with email masking (Built, waiting to be merged).** Login by one-time code with the email partly hidden. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* After merge, a cashier in Multan logs in with an OTP sent to a**@gmail.com.

**Pharmacy HR roles plan (Planned, not built yet).** A planned HR roles plan for pharmacies. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A future plan for HR roles in Lahore pharmacy chains.

**Role landing pages, dark mode (Planned, not built yet).** Planned landing pages per role and dark mode. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A night-shift pharmacist would land straight on the queue in dark mode.


### 7. Reports and finance

**Reports hub: replenishment, branch comparison, repeat purchasing, supplier credit follow-up (Available now).** Reports for replenishment, branch comparison, repeat buying and supplier credit follow-up. How it works: behind the screen the system has 25 server actions (15 that read and 10 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log; staff whose role carries the “reports:operational” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner, Owner / Operator, Auditor); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Key settings and limits checked in code: target sales share percent up to 100 characters or units, asserted description up to 10,000 characters or units, branch id up to 128 characters or units, lot id up to 128 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “A lot reference requires its medicine reference.”; “Inventory lot does not match this branch and medicine.”; “Report not found.”. *Example:* The owner of three Lahore branches compares sales and restocks the weakest one.

**Financial figures limited to people with `reports:financial` (Available now).** Money figures are only shown to people with the financial reports permission. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Faisalabad cashier sees unit counts but not profit.

**Finance: gross margin, margin comparison, leakage report, perpetual valuation, cash-flow forecast (Available now).** Gross margin, margin comparison, leakage, stock valuation and cash-flow forecast. How it works: behind the screen the system has 15 server actions (7 that read and 8 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; administrators who also pass an extra permission or branch check written inside that action. Key settings and limits checked in code: page up to 10,000 characters or units, reference up to 80 characters or units, counterparty name up to 160 characters or units, memo up to 500 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Choose a valuation instant no later than now.”; “Owner financial reports access is required.”; “Financial report permission is required.”. *Example:* The DHA owner sees Rs 90,000 margin this week and a leak from unrecorded discounts.

**Bookkeeping: post and reverse journal entries, period close and reopen, bank import and reconciliation, settlement batches, tax profile (Available now).** Bookkeeping: journals, period close/reopen, bank import and reconciliation, settlement batches and tax profile. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Lahore accountant imports the HBL statement and reconciles card settlements.

**Accounts: P&L, balance sheet, cash flow, trial balance, journal lookup and evidence export, cheque maturity, fiscal exceptions, tax-profile review, account activity bridge, account-code reference (Available now).** Accounting reports: P&L, balance sheet, cash flow, trial balance, journal evidence, cheque maturity, fiscal exceptions and tax profile review. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Gujranwala owner prints the monthly P&L for the bank.

**Report date controls, print/PDF, CSV export, filters (Built, waiting to be merged).** Report date controls, print/PDF, CSV export and filters. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* After merge, a Multan manager exports last month's sales as CSV.

**Account-code report (Built, waiting to be merged).** A report by account code. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* An accountant in Lahore checks totals per account code.

**Owner data export and import ("no lock-in" button) (Built, waiting to be merged).** Owner exports and imports all their data. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* After merge, a Sialkot owner could take a full export to switch systems.

Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A Model Town manager would see margins for Model Town only.

**Readable account names and journal (Built, waiting to be merged).** The trial balance will show readable names such as "Cost of goods sold" instead of internal codes such as COST_OF_GOODS_SOLD, and the journal will use shop language. The noisy tax report is still waiting to be started. *Example:* the accountant of a Lahore pharmacy reads "Sales clearing" instead of SALES_CLEARING when checking the month's trial balance.


### 8. Compliance (DRAP, controlled drugs, pharmacovigilance, FBR)

**Controlled-drug register, witnesses, controlled pharmacists (Available now).** Controlled-drug register with witnesses and named controlled-drug pharmacists. How it works: behind the screen the system has 11 server actions (6 that read and 5 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “pharmacy:safety” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner, Owner / Operator); the feature must also be switched on for the business; signed-in staff who also pass an extra permission or branch check written inside that action. Key settings and limits checked in code: correction reason up to 500 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “That register entry is not on this branch.”; “That correction would make the balance negative.”; “That correction is larger than the original quantity.”. *Example:* A Lahore hospital pharmacy records every tramadol sale with a witness.

**DRAP recalls (Available now).** DRAP recall handling. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Rawalpindi branch quarantines a recalled batch.

**Side-effect reports (pharmacovigilance): create, move through stages, record submission, export for DRAP (Available now; gap fixed).** Record a reported side effect or medication error, move it through review, record that it was submitted, and export it in DRAP's format. Filing needs the safety or dispensing permission. Reviewing, submitting and exporting need the safety permission itself, and the person must work at that branch. A pharmacy run by the owner alone can mark its own report ready after entering a fresh PIN, and that step is recorded. *Example:* a customer at a Township pharmacy reports a rash after a new antibiotic. The pharmacist files the report, the owner reviews it and exports it for DRAP, and the system keeps both names.

**Regulatory profile and onboarding gates (Available now).** The pharmacy's regulatory details and onboarding checks before go-live. How it works: behind the screen the system has 6 server actions (3 that read and 3 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only. Key settings and limits checked in code: note up to 1,000 characters or units, branch id up to 128 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Say where the evidence for this step is recorded.”. *Example:* A new Bahawalpur pharmacy enters its drug-sale licence before going live.

**FBR Tier-1 (Available now).** FBR Tier-1 retailer invoicing. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A large Lahore pharmacy sends sales invoices to FBR.

**Cameras: gateway, retention, viewer grants, evidence, privacy (Available now).** CCTV cameras: gateway, retention, who can view, evidence and privacy. How it works: behind the screen the system has 10 server actions (2 that read and 8 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only. Key settings and limits checked in code: name up to 120 characters or units, retention days up to 90 characters or units, username up to 200 characters or units, password up to 500 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Gateway unavailable in branch.”; “Private credential storage unavailable.”; “Viewer access expired or was revoked.”. *Example:* The DHA owner lets only the manager view the counter camera.

**Live CCTV view through your camera gateway (Available now, new; not yet tested on real cameras).** Once a small gateway box is set up at the pharmacy, staff can watch their cameras live inside DevFathom. Cameras start switched off. The DVR's password is never stored in DevFathom, and each viewing pass lasts under a minute and is checked again for every video segment. Only staff assigned to that branch, or the administrator, can watch, and DevFathom support staff can't. *Example:* the owner of a Gulberg pharmacy checks the counter camera from home at 11 pm to see that the shutter is down.

**Camera snapshots as evidence (Available now, new; not yet tested on real cameras).** When a sale is paid, a refund is made, the day is closed or a fridge goes out of range, the system can save a still photo from the right camera as evidence. A small service on the gateway takes one frame and never touches the recordings. *Example:* a disputed Rs 5,000 refund at a Model Town pharmacy comes with a still photo of the counter at the moment the refund was made.

**Hardware setup guide (Available now, new).** A checklist for testing cameras, receipt printers, scanners and card machines in the pharmacy. No device has passed yet; every row is marked untested. Receipt printer support and a card-machine connection are built and waiting for review. *Example:* before go-live in Rahim Yar Khan, the installer works through the guide and marks which printer and scanner actually worked.

**Licence expiry by local date (Built, waiting to be merged).** Licence expiry worked out by local date. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A Lahore licence expiring at midnight PKT is flagged on the right day.

**Emergency-supply limits by province (Built, waiting to be merged).** Will set how often a patient can get an emergency supply without a prescription, based on the province's rules. (A second, related change was withdrawn.) *Example:* in Punjab, a patient who already had an emergency supply this week would be told to bring a prescription.

**Postgres backup and restore drill (Built, waiting to be merged).** Database backup and restore drill. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* Developers would prove a Lahore pharmacy's data can be restored.

**Sindh profile (Built, waiting to be merged).** A Sindh regulatory profile. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A Karachi pharmacy would get Sindh-specific rules.

**Audit-actor immutability (Built, waiting to be merged).** Audit records can't be edited. Failed security due to a migration clash. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. *Known problem:* failed security (migration timestamp clash). Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* Once fixed, no one could change who did a Lahore refund in the audit log.


### 8b. Procedures (checklists with proof), new

Procedures are step-by-step checklists that staff complete with proof, such as a photo, a note or a link to a record the system already holds. **They never stop a sale or any other work; they only record that it was done.** They're switched on per branch. In new pharmacies, owners, Owner / Operators and managers can set them up and check them; pharmacists can complete and check them; and cashiers, purchasing and stock staff can complete them.

**Procedures screen (Available now, new).** One page shows today's board, the step-by-step runner, items waiting for checking, and setup. *Example:* the morning pharmacist at a Johar Town branch opens Procedures, sees "Opening" and "Temperature log" due, and works through them on the counter tablet.

**Ready-made procedure packs (Available now, new).** There are nine packs: opening, closing, temperature log, controlled-drug counts, expiry pull, receiving, hygiene, incident and corrective action, and training sign-off. Each comes with roles, triggers and the proof it needs. *Example:* a new pharmacy in Sargodha installs all nine packs on day one, so the closing checklist already asks for a photo of the locked shutter.

**Switch on a branch, assign procedures and roles (Available now, new).** A manager switches procedures on for a branch, chooses which procedures apply, and gives people procedure roles (manager, qualified person, assistant). Only someone with the dispensing permission can hold the qualified-person role. *Example:* the owner of a two-branch pharmacy in Lahore switches procedures on for DHA only, while Model Town keeps paper checklists for a month.

**Do a procedure and add proof (Available now, new).** A staff member claims a procedure, works through the steps, adds proof and submits. Some procedures can also be started on demand. Proof photos are kept as permanent receipts. *Example:* a cashier in Faisalabad completes the hygiene checklist and attaches a photo of the cleaned counter.

**Link records the system already has (Available now, new).** Instead of typing numbers again, staff can link a record the system already holds, such as a temperature reading or a goods receipt, within the right time window. Cancelled or reversed records can't be linked. If a linked fridge reading was out of range, the person must write what happened and what they did. *Example:* at closing in a Gulberg pharmacy, the pharmacist links the 9 pm fridge reading of 9°C and writes "door left open, moved insulin to the spare fridge".

**Independent checking and send-backs (Available now, new).** Someone else must check the work: you can't check your own procedure or give yourself a checking role. A checker can send work back, and the person must then add a note explaining the fix. *Example:* a Multan manager sends back a controlled-drug count because one line doesn't match the register. The pharmacist recounts and adds "miscounted one strip".

**Automatic scheduling (Available now, new; off by default).** Each branch can choose to have procedures created automatically at the times it sets. Nothing is assumed: no default times and no automatic escalation. *Example:* a Bahawalpur pharmacy sets opening for 8:30 am and closing for 11 pm, and the procedures appear on the board at those times each day.

**Procedures report and inspection pack (Available now, new).** A report shows what was done, late or checked, and the inspection pack has a procedures section to show an inspector. *Example:* when a Punjab drug inspector visits a Rawalpindi pharmacy, the owner prints the inspection pack with the last month's temperature logs and controlled-drug counts.

### 9. Growth and WhatsApp

**Incoming WhatsApp messages, including STOP (Available now).** The system receives WhatsApp messages from customers. When someone sends STOP, they're unsubscribed. A new test makes sure a repeated STOP doesn't send a second "you're unsubscribed" message. *Example:* a customer in Lahore replies STOP to a refill reminder. She gets one confirmation and no more reminders.

**Campaigns: templates, drafts, review, approve, preview, delivery history, sandbox, receipt attribution, AI assist (Available now).** Marketing campaigns via templates, with review, approval, preview, delivery history, sandbox, sales attribution and AI help. Any signed-in member can use it today. How it works: behind the screen the system has 19 server actions (7 that read and 12 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code). Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “AI insights are disabled. Manual campaigns remain available.”; “Attribution cutoff cannot be in the future.”. *Known problem:* Sev1 gap (module-only). *Example:* A Lahore pharmacy sends an approved Eid greeting and sees which receipts followed.

**Basket analysis and growth suggestions (generate and review) (Available now; should work once AI is switched on, not yet re-tested).** The system looks at what customers buy together and suggests growth ideas, which a person reviews before anything is sent. It used to show "unavailable" because no AI was connected. The new AI routing fixes that wherever DevFathom has switched AI on. Technical error messages no longer appear on the owner's screen. *Example:* a Gulberg pharmacy sees that customers who buy baby formula often buy diapers elsewhere, and reviews a suggestion to stock a better diaper range. (Campaigns never name a medicine.)

**Consent checked again just before sending (Built, waiting to be merged).** Before each WhatsApp message goes out, the system will check once more that the customer still agrees to receive messages. The replay test for STOP messages is already in; see above. *Example:* a customer in Sialkot unsubscribes at 6:59 pm, and the 7:00 pm campaign skips her.

**Back-in-stock WhatsApp (no drug name) (Built, waiting to be merged).** Back-in-stock WhatsApp without naming the drug. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* After merge, a customer in Sahiwal gets "your item is back" without the medicine name.

**Campaign cost preview plus a server-side drug-name block (Built, waiting to be merged).** Shows campaign cost first and blocks drug names on the server. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* After merge, a Lahore owner sees Rs 2,000 cost before sending.

**Bilingual order confirmation (Built, waiting to be merged).** Order confirmation in English and Urdu. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* After merge, a Rawalpindi customer gets the confirmation in both languages.


**Rule: campaigns never name a medicine (Drugs Act 1976 limits public drug advertising) (Planned, not built yet).** Planned rule that campaigns never name a medicine. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* A Multan pharmacy's promotion could say "pharmacy offers" but not a drug name.
