## 4. Restaurants

Card payments are demo-only for now, real printers haven't been tried yet, the FBR connection is coming soon, and text, email and WhatsApp alerts aren't switched on yet.

### Branches & settings

**List branches (Available now).** Shows all your restaurant outlets in one place. The owner uses it to see which branches are set up. For example, you check that Gulberg and DHA both appear. How it works: behind the screen the system has 5 server actions (2 that read and 3 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “restaurant:configure” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner / Administrator, General Manager); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “A branch with this name or code already exists.”; “Branch not found.”; “Activate the branch before making it primary.”.

**Create a branch (Available now).** Adds a new outlet so it can have its own tables, menu, staff and reports. Done by the owner. For example, you open a new outlet in Bahria Town and add it. How it works: behind the screen the system has 5 server actions (1 that read and 4 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “restaurant:configure” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner / Administrator, General Manager); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “A branch with this name or code already exists.”; “Branch not found.”; “Activate the branch before making it primary.”.

**Edit a branch (Available now).** Changes a branch's name, address or other details. Done by the owner or manager. For example, the branch moves to a new street and you update the address. How it works: behind the screen the system has 5 server actions (1 that read and 4 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “restaurant:configure” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner / Administrator, General Manager); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Branch not found.”; “A branch with this name or code already exists.”; “Activate the branch before making it primary.”.

**Activate / deactivate a branch (Available now).** Turns a branch on, or off when it closes, without losing its history. Done by the owner. For example, you switch off a branch that closed for renovation. How it works: behind the screen the system has 5 server actions (1 that read and 4 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “restaurant:configure” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner / Administrator, General Manager); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Branch not found.”; “Choose another primary branch before deactivating this one.”; “A branch with this name or code already exists.”.

**Branch settings screen (Available now).** The page where the owner manages all branches. For example, you open it to add or edit an outlet. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Branch commercial terms (Available now).** Sets business rules for each branch, such as limits on orders. Set by the owner. For example, one branch has a different limit on discounts than another. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Dashboard preferences (Available now).** Lets each user choose what appears on their home dashboard. For example, the owner puts today's sales at the top. How it works: behind the screen the system has 4 server actions (2 that read and 2 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Restaurant home dashboard (Available now).** The first page you see, with a quick view of the restaurant. Used by the owner and managers. For example, you open it each morning to see yesterday at a glance. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Business date (Available now).** Sales after midnight still count towards the right trading day. The owner sees cleaner daily reports. For example, a 1 am sale on Saturday counts as Friday night's business. How it works: Works out which trading day a sale belongs to, including after midnight. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Invite role fixes (Built, waiting to be merged).** This is not available yet. Fixes to inviting staff and to how their job titles show are being finished. How it works: Fixes to invite roles and role labels. PR #21. [PR #21]. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* at a café in Sargodha, a cashier uses “invite role fixes” once it is released.

**Void an item (Available now; approver rule tightened).** Remove an item after it was sent to the kitchen. If a void names an approver, that person must now enter their own PIN or badge, which the server checks (with a lockout after wrong tries), and they must have the approval permission at that branch. The person doing the void is taken from who is signed in, not from what the screen sends. A restaurant run by the owner alone can approve its own void after a fresh PIN. *Example:* a waiter at a Gulberg restaurant voids a wrongly ordered karahi. The shift manager taps in his own PIN to approve, and both names are recorded.

**Cancel an order (Available now; approver rule tightened).** Cancel the whole order. The same approver rule as voids applies: the approver's own PIN or badge, checked by the server. *Example:* a takeaway order at an MM Alam Road café is cancelled after the customer leaves. The manager approves it with his badge.

### Cash drawer & day close

**Open drawer session (Available now).** The cashier starts their shift by entering the starting cash in the drawer. This is a record in the system; it doesn't physically open the cash drawer. For example, the cashier starts the evening with Rs 5,000 in change. How it works: Start a shift with a float. This is a cash session, not a hardware drawer kick. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Drawer movement (Available now).** Records cash taken out or put into the drawer during a shift, with a reason. For example, Rs 1,000 taken out to buy ice. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Current drawer (Available now).** Shows how much cash should be in the drawer right now. For example, the manager checks mid-shift. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Close drawer (Available now).** The cashier counts the cash at the end of the shift and the system shows any difference. For example, the count is Rs 200 short and it's recorded. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Day close preview (Available now).** Shows the day's totals before the manager closes the day. For example, you check sales before closing. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Close the day (Available now).** Closes the trading day and locks in the totals. Done by the manager. For example, the manager closes the day at midnight. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Correct a day close (Available now).** Fixes a mistake in a day that was already closed, keeping a record of the change. For example, a missed cash payment is added to yesterday. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Close readiness (Available now).** Shows what still needs doing before the day can be closed. For example, two tables are still open, so you can't close yet. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Drawer / close history (Available now).** Shows past cash shifts and day closes. For example, you check last Friday's cash count. How it works: behind the screen the system has 8 server actions that only read data, each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “money:read” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner / Administrator, General Manager, Accountant); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Key settings and limits checked in code: limit up to 100 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “The drawer session does not exist.”; “The daily close does not exist.”.

### Kitchen display (KDS)

**KDS screen (Available now).** A screen in the kitchen shows new orders instead of paper slips, so kitchen staff can see what to cook. It refreshes every few moments rather than instantly. For example, the chef sees Table 4's order appear. How it works: Kitchen screen showing tickets. Updates by polling (no live push channel). Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**KDS queue (Available now).** Shows the orders waiting to be cooked, in order. For example, the chef sees three orders waiting. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Acknowledge a ticket (Available now).** The kitchen taps to show they've seen an order. For example, the grill chef accepts Table 4's order. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Bump (Available now).** The kitchen taps an order off the screen when it's ready. For example, the burgers are done, so the chef clears the order. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Recall (Available now).** Brings back an order that was cleared by mistake. For example, the chef cleared the wrong order and brings it back. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**KDS operators (Available now).** Shows who is working on the kitchen screen. For example, you see which chef is on the grill screen. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Delivery board (Available now).** A screen showing delivery orders and their progress. For example, the dispatcher sees which orders are ready to go out. **Device list (Available now).** Shows all the tills and devices registered to your restaurant. For example, you check all three tills are listed. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Active branch not found.”; “Branch not found.”; “KDS station is required.”.

**Enrol a print bridge (Available now).** Connects the small printer program on your restaurant computer to the system. Done once by the manager. For example, you connect the counter computer that runs the printers. **Local print bridge (ESC/POS) (Available now).** A small program on a computer in your restaurant sends receipts and kitchen slips to your printers, whether they're connected by cable or over the network. It hasn't been tried on real printers yet, and it can't open the cash drawer automatically. For example, the kitchen slip would print as soon as the waiter sends the order. How it works: A program run on the shop PC (Windows script included) that sends ESC/POS jobs to a network, USB or serial, or installed-queue printer. Not tested on real hardware, and no cash-drawer kick. [scripts/print-bridge.ts, print-bridge-windows.ps1; src/lib/restaurant/printing/target.ts:4-6, printing/esc-pos.ts]. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

**Server-side print simulation (Available now).** If no printer program is set up in the restaurant, the system only pretends to print and keeps a record of what would have printed. That's useful for testing, but nothing comes out on paper. How it works: Without a bridge, the server only simulates printing (jobs are stored in the DB). Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* at a café in Sargodha, a cashier uses “server-side print simulation”.

Who uses it: signed-in staff who also pass an extra permission or branch check written inside that action; staff whose role carries the “devices:manage” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner / Administrator); the feature must also be switched on for the business. ### AI growth features

**AI growth recommendations: loyalty review (Available now).** The system suggests ways to reward loyal customers, based on how many are eligible. The owner approves or rejects each suggestion. For example, it suggests a reward for 120 regulars. How it works: AI suggests loyalty actions, backed by the count of eligible members. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Insight could not be saved. Reconcile the same request before trying another.”; “Review could not be completed. Reload the saved result before another decision.”.

**AI growth recommendations: catering review (Available now).** The system suggests ways to grow your catering business, based on your confirmed events. For example, it suggests following up with past event clients. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

For example, it flags dishes with no recipe cost. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row.

## 5. Agencies (studios and firms)

### Packages, modules & roles

**Agency Core module (Available now).** The basic package every agency gets, and it's always on. It includes the dashboard, team, chat, notes, meetings, email and HR basics. How it works: Always on: dashboard, team, chat, notes, meetings, email and HR basics. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A digital agency in Gulberg, Lahore gets team, chat, notes and HR on day one with the Core module.

**Projects module (switchable) (Available now).** Projects, task boards and time tracking. It can be turned on or off depending on the plan the agency pays for. How it works: Projects, boards, tasks and time tracking. It can be switched on or off per plan. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Johar Town software studio switches on Projects to run its client app builds.

**Clients module (switchable) (Available now).** Client records, contact people and contracts. It can be turned on or off depending on the plan. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* An architecture firm in DHA switches on Clients to keep its housing-society clients in one place.

**Finance module (switchable) (Available now).** Invoices, income and expenses, budgets and profit-and-loss. It can be turned on or off depending on the plan. How it works: Invoices, transactions, expenses, budgets and P&L. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Faisalabad marketing agency switches on Finance to send invoices to textile-mill clients.

**Seat-based plans (Available now).** Agency plans are priced by the number of staff logins. There are no other limits, so a 10-person agency simply pays for 10 seats. How it works: Agency plans count users only. There are no other agency limits. ]. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A 12-person Lahore studio pays for 12 seats and adds a 13th when a new designer joins.

**Ready-made roles: Administrator and Member (Available now).** Two standard roles come ready. An Administrator can do everything, and a Member has the basic access only. These roles may still change. How it works: Administrator has every permission; Member has none extra. Marked provisional. There are no agency job roles like designer or account manager. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The founder of a Model Town agency is Administrator; the designers are Members.

**Agency departments (Available now).** Staff can be grouped into Leadership, Engineering, Product, Quality, Marketing and Design. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Lahore agency gets Design, Development and Accounts departments ready-made.

**Shared platform (login, 2FA, SSO, roles, tenancy, billing, audit) (Available now).** These work the same as in the pharmacy product. See the pharmacy feature list for details. How it works: Same as pharmacy. See the Platform rows in devfathom-pharmacy-features-detailed.md. [devfathom-pharmacy-features-detailed.md]. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). **Agency growth / marketing / AI tools (Planned, not built yet).** The marketing, loyalty and smart-suggestion tools that pharmacies and restaurants have aren't included for agencies. This isn't available yet. How it works: There are none for agencies. Growth, loyalty and AI actions are wired only for pharmacy and restaurant. **Create invoice (Available now; numbering fixed).** Make an invoice for a client with line items, in US dollars or rupees. Each agency now numbers its own invoices (INV-2026-001, INV-2026-002 and so on). Two agencies can both have INV-2026-001, and two people creating invoices at the same moment can't get the same number. Before this, a second agency's first invoice of the year could fail. *Example:* a design studio in Gulberg and a marketing agency in DHA each send their first invoice of 2026 as INV-2026-001, and both save without an error.

Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “Invoice not found”; “Invoice status changed concurrently. Refresh and try again.”; “Payment must be at least 0.01.”. *Example:* The founder reviews the quarterly profit and loss.

**Transactions REST API (Available now).** Lets other software read and add income and expense records. This is for connecting other software. Your tech person would use it, not everyday staff. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* An outside tool reads transactions through the API.

**Finance summary REST API (Available now).** Lets other software read your money summary. This is for connecting other software. Your tech person would use it, not everyday staff. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A dashboard pulls the finance summary through the API.

**Export reports to spreadsheet (Planned, not built yet).** Downloading finance reports into Excel. This isn't available yet. How it works: No export button found for finance reports (TODO(confirm) in the agency guide). Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* The accounts officer exports reports to a spreadsheet for the auditor.

## 6. Hotels

So far only the setup side is built: property details, rooms and beds, meal plans, extras, halls and guest message templates. Bookings, check-in, guest bills, housekeeping and room prices are planned or in review, and hotel work is paused for now.

### Packages, modes & feature switches

**Hotel Essentials package (Available now).** A plan for a single property with up to 50 rooms and 15 staff logins. It's meant for a small hotel or guest house. Today only the setup screens work. Bookings, front desk and billing, which this plan is meant to include, aren't available yet. How it works: Package for one property, up to 50 rooms and 15 users, with core, reservations, front desk, folio, payments, night audit and reports modules. Only the core settings work today; the other modules are switches with no screens yet. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A 30-room hotel in Gulberg, Lahore starts on Essentials with one property.

**Hotel Pro package (Available now).** A bigger plan for up to 3 properties, 250 rooms and 75 staff. It's for a small hotel group. The extra things it's meant to include, like online channels, marketing and travel agents, aren't available yet. How it works: Up to 3 properties, 250 rooms and 75 users, adding channels, upsell, marketing, travel agents, maintenance and partner API modules (all still Planned). Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A business hotel on Mall Road, Lahore uses Pro for more rooms and features.

**Hotel Chain package (Available now).** The largest plan, for up to 100 properties. It's for a hotel chain that wants one central booking desk, but that central desk isn't available yet. How it works: Up to 100 properties, 10,000 rooms and 2,500 users, adding central reservations (Planned). Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A group with hotels in Lahore, Murree and Bhurban uses the Chain package.

**Venue package (Available now).** A plan for places that only rent out halls and event spaces, with no guest rooms. A marriage hall, for example, can set up its halls and hold time slots on them. How it works: For event venues with no rooms: function spaces and banquets, room limit deliberately 0. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A wedding hall in DHA, Lahore uses the Venue package for events only.

**Hotel limits (properties, rooms, users) (Available now).** The system automatically keeps each customer within the properties, rooms and staff their plan allows. If a limit is ever missing or wrong, it plays safe and allows nothing rather than everything. A 50-room plan, for example, can't add a 51st room. How it works: The plan's limits are read safely: a missing or invalid limit counts as zero, never as unlimited. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Lahore hotel on Essentials is told when it reaches its room or user limit.

**Property mode: Hotel (Available now).** The standard setting for a normal hotel, where rooms are sold by the night. New properties start in this mode unless you choose another. How it works: Rooms sold by the night, with a front desk and nightly audit. It is the default mode. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Faisalabad business hotel runs in Hotel mode.

**Property mode: Hostel (Available now).** For hostels that sell single beds in shared rooms. A room counts as full when its beds are taken, so a 6-bed dorm can be sold one bed at a time. How it works: Beds sold one by one in shared rooms; room occupancy comes from the beds. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A backpacker hostel near Anarkali, Lahore runs in Hostel mode with beds.

**Property mode: Serviced apartments (Available now).** For apartments rented by the week or month. It's built for longer stays with regular charges, like monthly cleaning. How it works: Units let by the week or month, with recurring charges and tenancy documents. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* Serviced apartments in Bahria Town, Lahore run in apartment mode.

**Property mode: Resort (Available now).** For resorts that sell overnight stays and also day passes on the same rooms. A family could book a room just for the afternoon, for example. How it works: Rooms by the night plus day-use slots on the same rooms. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A resort in Murree runs in Resort mode.

**Property mode: Venue (Available now).** For event spaces only, with no guest rooms. A banquet hall would use this. How it works: Function spaces and events only, with no rooms. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A marquee in Johar Town runs in Venue mode.

**Mode defaults (Available now).** When you pick a property type, the system fills in sensible starting settings for you. You can change them afterwards. Picking Hostel, for example, sets it up to sell beds. How it works: Choosing a mode fills in sensible defaults (what is sold, billing cycles, booking layout) that the owner can change. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Lahore hostel starts with bed-based settings by default.

**Mode overrides (Available now).** The owner can fine-tune what a property sells (rooms, beds or apartments), how guests are charged (nightly, weekly, monthly or by time slot), and other rules. A hotel that also rents a few apartments monthly could turn that on here. How it works: Change what a property sells (rooms, beds, units), its billing cycles (nightly, weekly, monthly, slot), dorm rules, recurring extras, tenancy documents and booking-engine layout. Saved per property. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code); staff with access to this part of the system (gate set by a shared helper: result). Key settings and limits checked in code: user id up to 128 characters or units, organization role id up to 128 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “You cannot grant yourself property access.”; “Active member or Hotel role not found.”; “Another authorized manager must change your assignment.”. *Example:* A Murree resort turns on day-use even though its mode doesn't by default.

**Day-use slot calculation (Available now).** The system can work out the time window for a short daytime room booking and won't use rooms marked out of order. There's no screen to actually book a day-use slot yet. How it works: Works out a day-use slot's time window on a room, and refuses rooms that are out of order. This is a calculation only, because there's no booking screen yet. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Lahore airport hotel works out 6-hour day-use slots.


### Property setup & settings

**Hotel landing page (Available now).** The hotel home screen shows each property you look after, what type it is, and whether it's ready to go live. If you haven't been given a property yet, it tells you so. How it works: The /hotel home shows the properties you manage, their mode and how many are ready to go live, or tells you that no property is assigned to you. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The general manager in Gulberg opens the hotel landing page.

**Property settings page (Available now).** The screen where a manager enters the property's basic facts and house rules. This is usually the first thing you fill in when setting up. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The GM opens property settings.

**View property (Available now).** Shows the property's saved details so a manager can check them at any time. How it works: behind the screen the system has 13 server actions (7 that read and 6 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code); staff with access to this part of the system (gate set by a shared helper: result). Key settings and limits checked in code: user id up to 128 characters or units, organization role id up to 128 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “You cannot grant yourself property access.”; “Active member or Hotel role not found.”; “Another authorized manager must change your assignment.”. *Example:* Reception views the property's address and check-in time.

**Edit property facts (Available now).** A manager can update the property's name, time zone, check-in and check-out times, and its daily closing time. For example, you can change check-out from 12:00 to 11:00. How it works: Update name, timezone, check-in and check-out times, business date, audit time and policies. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code); staff with access to this part of the system (gate set by a shared helper: result). Key settings and limits checked in code: user id up to 128 characters or units, organization role id up to 128 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “You cannot grant yourself property access.”; “Active member or Hotel role not found.”; “Another authorized manager must change your assignment.”. *Example:* The GM changes check-in time to 2 pm.

**Timezone check (Available now).** The system won't accept a time zone that doesn't exist, which stops typing mistakes that would mess up dates. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The system checks the property's time zone is Asia/Karachi.

**Booking-engine web address (slug) (Available now).** Each property gets a short web name ready for its own online booking page in future. The booking page itself isn't available yet. How it works: Each property gets a short web name for a future booking page, generated from the organization and property names. The booking page itself doesn't exist yet. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Lahore hotel reserves "pc-gulberg" as its future booking-page address.

**Policy documents (Available now).** A place to save house rules like cancellation and deposit policies. Later parts of the system will use them. Nothing enforces them yet. How it works: Space to store cancellation, deposit and similar policies as free-form settings. Later modules will use them. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The GM saves the cancellation and deposit policy.

**Tax profile (Available now).** A manager enters the property's tax details: province, room tax rate, whether prices include tax, any fixed charges, and tax registration numbers. This only checks the details are filled in correctly. It doesn't confirm you meet the law. How it works: Set the province (Punjab, Sindh, KP, Balochistan, ICT, GB, AJK), room tax rate, whether prices include or exclude tax, fixed levies, and the NTN and STRN numbers. It checks the setup only and doesn't certify legal readiness. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The GM enters Punjab tax details and the PRA registration number.

**Room-night tax calculation (Available now).** The system can work out the exact tax on one night's stay. It isn't used on guest bills yet, because guest billing isn't available. How it works: Calculates the tax on one room-night in whole paisa, using integer maths. Nothing calls it for billing yet, because there's no folio on develop. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The system works out tax on one Rs 15,000 room night.

**Hotel e-invoicing (FBR) (Available now).** There's a switch for sending invoices to the tax authority, but it only runs in practice mode. Nothing is actually sent to FBR yet. How it works: The tax profile has an e-invoice switch and integration field, but submission defaults to SIMULATED. There's no live FBR integrator. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Lahore hotel switches on FBR e-invoicing in practice mode.

**Go-live checklist (Available now).** Six steps every property must finish before it can go live: property type, basic facts, tax details, daily closing time, staff roles, and at least one room type (or one hall for a venue). It shows what's done and exactly what's left. How it works: Six steps a property must finish before activation: mode, property facts, tax profile, business date and audit time, roles assigned, and at least one room type (or function space for a venue). Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The GM works through the go-live checklist.


**Deactivate / reactivate a property (Available now).** Our team can switch a property off, for example when the customer stops using it, and switch it back on later. Before switching it back on, the system checks again that setup is complete. How it works: Platform staff can switch a property off and back on. Reactivation re-checks the checklist. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A seasonal Murree resort is deactivated in winter and reactivated in spring.

**Recover property manager (Available now).** If a property ends up with no manager who can get in, our team can put one back. This saves a customer from being locked out. How it works: Platform staff can restore a manager to a property that has lost every admin. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* DevFathom restores access when a Lahore hotel's manager leaves.


**Change history (audit) (Available now).** Every change to hotel settings is recorded, with what it was before and after and who changed it. If a room rate setting changes, you can see who changed it and when. How it works: Every hotel settings change is written to the audit log with before and after values. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The GM sees who changed the tax rate.

Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Lahore hostel sets up 8 beds in a dorm.

Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code). ### Meal plans & extras

**Meal plans page (Available now).** The screen where a manager sets up meal packages for guests. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The F&B manager opens the meal plans page.

**Default meal plans (Available now).** Every new property starts with the four standard packages: room only, with breakfast, with two meals, and with all meals. How it works: New properties start with the standard plans: EP (room only), CP (breakfast), MAP (two meals), AP (all meals). Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A Lahore hotel gets Room Only and Bed & Breakfast by default.

**List meal plans (Available now).** Shows the property's meal packages. How it works: behind the screen the system has 4 server actions (1 that read and 3 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “hotel:rates” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner, Front Office Manager, Revenue / Marketing Manager); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* Reception lists the meal plans.

**Create meal plan (Available now).** A manager adds a meal package and picks which meals it covers and the last serving times. You also choose whether meals are per room or per guest. An Urdu name can be added. How it works: Add a plan, choosing which of breakfast, lunch and dinner it covers, service cut-off times, and whether meals belong to the room or each guest. An Urdu name is optional. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “hotel:rates” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner, Front Office Manager, Revenue / Marketing Manager); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The GM creates "Half board" with dinner.

**Edit meal plan (Available now).** A manager changes a meal package. Its short code stays the same because it appears on vouchers. How it works: Change a plan. Its code can't change, because it identifies the plan on vouchers. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “hotel:rates” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner, Front Office Manager, Revenue / Marketing Manager); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The price of breakfast is changed.

**Switch meal plan on or off (Available now).** Stop offering a meal package without deleting it, so its history is kept. How it works: behind the screen the system has 4 server actions (1 that read and 3 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “hotel:rates” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner, Front Office Manager, Revenue / Marketing Manager); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* "Full board" is switched off in the off-season.

**Extras page (Available now).** The screen where a manager lists paid extras guests can buy. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The GM opens the extras page.

**List extras (Available now).** Shows the list of paid extras. How it works: behind the screen the system has 12 server actions (6 that read and 6 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code); staff with access to this part of the system (gate set by a shared helper: result). Key settings and limits checked in code: user id up to 128 characters or units, organization role id up to 128 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “You cannot grant yourself property access.”; “Active member or Hotel role not found.”; “Another authorized manager must change your assignment.”. *Example:* Reception lists extras such as airport pickup.

**Create extra (Available now).** A manager adds an extra like airport pickup, laundry or a spa session. You set its price in rupees, its tax type, and whether it's charged once, every night, once per stay or per person. How it works: Add an extra (for example airport pickup) with a price in PKR, tax class, department and how often it charges: one off, every night, once per stay or per person. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code); staff with access to this part of the system (gate set by a shared helper: result). Key settings and limits checked in code: user id up to 128 characters or units, organization role id up to 128 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “You cannot grant yourself property access.”; “Active member or Hotel role not found.”; “Another authorized manager must change your assignment.”. *Example:* The GM adds "Airport pickup – Rs 3,500".

**Edit extra (Available now).** A manager changes an extra's price or details. How it works: behind the screen the system has 12 server actions (6 that read and 6 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code); staff with access to this part of the system (gate set by a shared helper: result). Key settings and limits checked in code: user id up to 128 characters or units, organization role id up to 128 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “You cannot grant yourself property access.”; “Active member or Hotel role not found.”; “Another authorized manager must change your assignment.”. *Example:* The pickup price is updated.

**Switch extra on or off (Available now).** Stop offering an extra without deleting it. How it works: behind the screen the system has 12 server actions (6 that read and 6 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: administrators of the business only; staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business; any signed-in staff member of this business (the action itself names no role; some add branch checks in shared helper code); staff with access to this part of the system (gate set by a shared helper: result). Key settings and limits checked in code: user id up to 128 characters or units, organization role id up to 128 characters or units. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “You cannot grant yourself property access.”; “Active member or Hotel role not found.”; “Another authorized manager must change your assignment.”. *Example:* Late checkout is switched off on busy weekends.

**Extra/billing-cycle check (Available now).** The system blocks extras that don't fit how a guest is charged, like a nightly charge on a monthly apartment rental. It explains why in plain words. How it works: Refuses pairings that make no sense (for example a nightly extra on a monthly let), with a reason the owner can act on. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The system checks an extra's billing cycle is valid.

**Posting departments (Available now).** Each extra is filed under an area such as Rooms, Food and Beverage, Laundry, Spa or Transport. That will let future reports show where money comes from. How it works: Extras are filed under 14 departments (Rooms, Day use, F&B, Minibar, Laundry, Spa, Transport and others) for future reports. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* Charges are tagged to Restaurant, Laundry or Rooms departments.

**Rate plans (nightly prices + tax rules) (Built, waiting to be merged).** Setting nightly room prices and pricing rules. This isn't available yet. ### Function spaces & events

**Spaces page (Available now).** The screen where a manager sets up halls and meeting rooms. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The events manager opens the spaces page.

**List function spaces (Available now).** Shows all halls and meeting rooms. How it works: behind the screen the system has 8 server actions (3 that read and 5 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “That hold is no longer here, or it is a confirmed function, which is released from its event rather than from the space.”. *Example:* She lists the ballroom and two meeting rooms.

**Create function space (Available now).** A manager adds a hall with how many people it holds in different layouts (theatre or banquet style) and its price. You can also add time needed to set up and clear away. An Urdu name can be added. How it works: Add a space with capacity per layout (theatre, banquet and so on), pricing, setup and clear-down buffers, and an optional Urdu name. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “That hold is no longer here, or it is a confirmed function, which is released from its event rather than from the space.”. *Example:* She adds the "Shalimar Hall" for 300 guests.

**Edit function space (Available now).** A manager changes a hall's details. How it works: behind the screen the system has 8 server actions (2 that read and 6 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “That hold is no longer here, or it is a confirmed function, which is released from its event rather than from the space.”. *Example:* She changes the hall's capacity.

**Switch space on or off (Available now).** Stop offering a hall, for example during renovation, without deleting it. How it works: behind the screen the system has 8 server actions (2 that read and 6 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “That hold is no longer here, or it is a confirmed function, which is released from its event rather than from the space.”. *Example:* A meeting room is switched off for renovation.

**Space availability (Available now).** Shows when a hall is free, including the setup and clear-away time, so you can see at a glance whether the main hall is free on Saturday evening. How it works: See when a space is free in a time window, counting buffers. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “That hold is no longer here, or it is a confirmed function, which is released from its event rather than from the space.”. *Example:* She checks if the ballroom is free on a Saturday in December.

**List space bookings (Available now).** Shows the time slots held on a hall. How it works: behind the screen the system has 7 server actions (2 that read and 5 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “That hold is no longer here, or it is a confirmed function, which is released from its event rather than from the space.”. *Example:* She lists the month's space bookings.

**Hold a space (Available now).** A manager reserves a hall for a time slot of up to a week. The system won't let the same hall be held twice at the same time. How it works: Reserve a space for a time slot, up to a week. Double-booking is refused by the database. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “That hold is no longer here, or it is a confirmed function, which is released from its event rather than from the space.”. *Example:* She puts a 48-hour hold on the hall for a mehndi enquiry.

**Release a space hold (Available now).** Cancels a hold so the hall is free again. How it works: behind the screen the system has 7 server actions (2 that read and 5 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: staff whose role carries the “hotel:property-admin” permission (in the ready-made roles: Owner); the feature must also be switched on for the business. Limits the system enforces (its own messages): “That hold is no longer here, or it is a confirmed function, which is released from its event rather than from the space.”. *Example:* The hold is released when the family books elsewhere.

**Banquet event orders, catering and menus (Planned, not built yet).** Full event management with event orders, catering menus and event billing. This isn't available yet. How it works: Full banquets module: event orders, catering and billing. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* Planned: a wedding's banquet order, catering and menu.


### Guest journey messages

**Journey settings page (Available now).** The screen where a manager writes the messages guests will receive during their stay. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The GM opens journey settings.

**Eight journey moments (Available now).** Message templates for eight moments: booking confirmed, an offer before arrival, self check-in, welcome, during the stay, express check-out, invoice and review request. How it works: Templates for booking confirmed, pre-arrival offer, self check-in, welcome, during the stay, express checkout, invoice and review request. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* Messages for 8 moments such as pre-arrival and checkout.

**Channels and languages (Available now).** Each message can be written for WhatsApp, email or SMS, in English or Urdu. How it works: Each template is for WhatsApp, e-mail or SMS, in English or Urdu. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* Messages by WhatsApp or email, in English or Urdu.

**List templates (Available now).** Shows all guest messages and whether each is a draft, approved or retired. How it works: behind the screen the system has 6 server actions (3 that read and 3 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* The GM lists guest message templates.

**Save draft template (Available now).** A manager writes a message with fill-in spots like the guest's name. The system catches broken fill-in spots before saving. How it works: Write or edit a template using placeholders like the guest name. Broken placeholders are refused. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* She saves a draft welcome message.

**Approve template (Available now).** A senior manager approves a draft message before it can be used, so nothing goes out unchecked. How it works: A manager approves a draft before it can be used. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* A manager approves the template.

**Retire template (Available now).** Stops using a message without deleting it. How it works: behind the screen the system has 6 server actions (2 that read and 4 that save changes), each tied to this business and its branches. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* An old template is retired.

**Preview template (Available now).** Shows how the message will look with sample guest details filled in. How it works: See the message with sample guest details filled in. Who uses it: any signed-in staff member of the business, with every use written to the audit log. Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* She previews the message with a sample guest.

**Marketing vs service consent (Available now).** Offers and review requests count as marketing, so they'll only go to guests who agreed to marketing. Practical messages, like a booking confirmation, are treated separately. How it works: Pre-arrival offers and review requests count as marketing and need marketing consent. The rest are service messages. Who uses it: staff of the business with access to this screen (the code behind this row is a page or shared setting, not a single permission check). Limits: no extra limits beyond the permission check were found in the code for this row. *Example:* Guests' marketing and service consents are kept separate.

**Actually sending journey messages (Planned, not built yet).** These messages can be written and approved, but nothing sends them yet. There are no bookings to trigger them, and no WhatsApp, SMS or email sending for hotels yet. How it works: Nothing sends these templates yet. There's no WhatsApp, SMS or e-mail sending for hotel, and no stays to trigger them. Who uses it: nobody yet, because this is not on the main code line. Limits: it does not work in the live product until the change is merged and released. *Example:* Messages aren't actually sent yet.

## 7. Other kinds of business

DevFathom currently supports only these four kinds of business. There's also a demo restaurant for sales demos, and a secure link that lets a supplier open a purchase order without logging in. *Example:* a medicine distributor on McLeod Road opens the order link from WhatsApp and sees what the pharmacy wants.
